<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the diagonal Fourier extension conjecture for compact smooth positively curved surfaces $`\Sigma\subset\mathbb R^3`$, including surfaces with smooth boundary. For every $`3\lt p\lt \infty`$, the extension operator maps $`L^p(\Sigma)`$ boundedly into $`L^p(\mathbb R^3)`$. The compact paraboloid case also yields free Schrödinger local smoothing in two spatial dimensions for every $`3\lt p\lt \infty`$ and Sobolev order $`s\gt 2-6/p`$.

- **claim-diagonal-surface-extension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $\Sigma\subset\mathbb R^3$ be a compact smooth positively curved surface, possibly with smooth boundary. For every $3<p<\infty$, there is a finite constant $C_{\Sigma,p}$ such that $$\label{eq:main} \\|E_\Sigma f\\|_{L^p(\mathbb R^3)} \le C_{\Sigma,p}\\|f\\|_{L^p(\Sigma)}$$ for every complex-valued $f\in L^p(\Sigma)$.
  - Explanation: The surface extension operator is bounded at every finite exponent strictly above the critical exponent, including for surfaces with smooth boundary.
- **claim-free-schrodinger-smoothing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-schrodinger-smoothing)): For every $3<p<\infty$ and $s>2-6/p$, the free Schrödinger propagator, initially defined on Schwartz functions, extends boundedly from $W^{s,p}(\mathbb R^2)$ to $L^p(\mathbb R^2\times[0,1])$: $$\label{eq:schrodinger-smoothing} \\|e^{it\Delta}f\\|_{L^p(\mathbb R^2\times[0,1])} \le C_{p,s}\\|f\\|_{W^{s,p}(\mathbb R^2)}.$$
  - Explanation: Compact paraboloid extension transfers to a spacetime estimate for free Schrödinger evolution; strict Sobolev regularity makes the frequency-scale summation converge.
- **claim-elliptic-graph-insertion** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-continuation)): Every germ of a smooth graph with positive definite Hessian can be carried by an invertible affine transformation of $\mathbb R^3$ to a graph agreeing, on an open neighborhood, with a phase of the form in Equation <a href="#eq:spherical-continuation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:spherical-continuation">[eq:spherical-continuation]</a>. The phase, the neighborhood, and the affine transformation can all be fixed independently of the packet scales and of the exponents used below.
  - Explanation: Matching Taylor data inside the transition annulus inserts a general elliptic graph into the precise continuation class required by packet propagation.
- **claim-exact-packet-frame** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-packet-frame)): For every $f\in L^2(\mathbb R^2)$, $$\label{eq:packet-frame} \mathsf S_{m,I}\mathsf P_{m,I}f=f, \qquad \\|\mathsf P_{m,I}f\\|_{\ell^2}^2 =(D_{\mathrm f}\theta)^2\\|f\\|_2^2, \qquad \\|\mathsf S_{m,I}\\|_{\ell^2\to L^2} =(D_{\mathrm f}\theta)^{-1}.$$
  - Explanation: Fourier inversion and the square-window partition give exact reconstruction and the analysis and synthesis normalizations, with convergence in the stated function space.
- **claim-atomic-norm-sample-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-packet-atomic)): The function $A_{\theta,r}$ is a norm on every finite coordinate space, is monotone under absolute coordinatewise domination, and is contracted by coordinate masks. Moreover, $$\label{eq:packet-atomic-lower} rw^2\sum_{\nu,z,I}\|c_{\nu,z,I}\|^3\le\mathcal A_m(c)^3.$$
  - Explanation: The atomic domination construction produces a genuine norm whose weighted cubic lower bound survives coordinate restriction.
- **claim-masked-packet-propagation** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-packet)): *For a fixed phase as in Equation <a href="#eq:spherical-continuation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:spherical-continuation">[eq:spherical-continuation]</a>, every fixed admissible complexity range, $0<\kappa<1/10$, and $\eta>0$, there is a finite constant $C$ such that $$\label{eq:packet-source} \mathcal A_m(T_md)^3 \le C m^{10\kappa+\eta}G_{\delta,1}(d)^3$$ for all its single-step maps and all its finite complex input arrays. The source also permits $A_{\delta,1}(d)^3$ on the right. The constant may depend on $\kappa$, $\eta$, the complexity range, the phase, and the windows.* *In particular, suppose the initial labels $\nu\in\delta\mathbb Z^2$ and $z\in D_{\mathrm f}^{-1}\delta\mathbb Z^2$ lie in fixed bounded sets. Define $$\begin{aligned} S d(\xi) &=(D_{\mathrm f}\delta)^{-2} \sum_{\nu,z}d_{\nu,z}\chi_\delta^\nu(\xi) e(-N^2z\cdot\xi), \label{eq:packet-synthesis}\\ c_{\nu',z',j} &=\int Sd(\xi)\chi_1^{\nu'}(\xi) e\bigl(N^2z'\cdot\xi+j\phi(\xi)\bigr)\,d\xi, \label{eq:packet-samples} \end{aligned}$$ where $\nu'\in\mathbb Z^2$, $z'\in D_{\mathrm f}^{-1}N^{-2}\mathbb Z^2$, and $j=0,\ldots,N^2-1$. Retain any output coordinate mask for which $\nu',z'$ lie in fixed bounded sets. There is a finite $P(\kappa,\eta)\ge1$, depending on these fixed sets, the phase, and the windows, such that, for every dyadic $N\ge2$ and $0<\eta\le1$, $$\label{eq:packet-bound} N^{-6}\sum_{\nu',z',j}\|c_{\nu',z',j}\|^3 \le P(\kappa,\eta)N^{10\kappa+\eta} m(d)\mathcal K_\kappa(d)^{1/2}.$$ The sum is over the retained coordinates.*
  - Explanation: The imported propagation theorem controls arbitrary finite complex input arrays through the initial capacity gauge, with constants depending on the fixed phase, windows, complexity range, and loss parameters.
- **claim-weighted-kakeya-duality** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-weighted)): There are fixed constants $C$ and $N_0$ such that for every dyadic $N\ge N_0$, every initial array $d$, and every $0<s\le1$, $$\label{eq:weighted-tubes} \int H_d^{3/2} \le C M(s)^{3/2}\delta^{-3s/2} \delta^2\sum_\nu b_\nu(d)^{3/2}.$$ The constants $C,N_0$ depend only on the fixed geometric setup.
  - Explanation: Separated direction caps and maximal-operator duality bound positive weighted tube density without an additional loss from position labels.
- **claim-capacity-level-decomposition** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-density)): Let $d$ be a nonzero finite initial array, and let $n=\#\operatorname{supp}d$. For every $0<\kappa<1/10$, there is a decomposition into coordinate subarrays with pairwise disjoint supports, $$d=\sum_{i=1}^{J}d^{(i)},\qquad J\le C\log(2+n),$$ such that $$\label{eq:density-cost} \sum_{i=1}^{J}m(d^{(i)})\mathcal K_\kappa(d^{(i)})^{1/2} \le C\int H_d^{3/2}.$$ Both constants are absolute and independent of the nonzero coefficient sizes and of $\kappa$.
  - Explanation: Selecting near-maximal ellipse tests and grouping by remainder capacity yields disjoint pieces with logarithmic cardinality and controlled total cost, even when coefficient magnitudes vary arbitrarily.
- **claim-discrete-critical-sample-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-discrete)): Let $c$ be the terminal coefficient array obtained from $d$ by the synthesis and sampling map of Theorem <a href="#thm:packet" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:packet">6</a>, with any fixed permitted output mask. For every sufficiently large dyadic $N$, $0<\kappa<1/10$, $0<\eta\le1$, and $0<s\le1$, put $\gamma_0=10\kappa+\eta+3s/2$. Then $$\label{eq:discrete-critical} \sum_{\nu',z',j}\|c_{\nu',z',j}\|^3 \le C N^6 P(\kappa,\eta)M(s)^{3/2} N^{\gamma_0}\log^2(2+N) \delta^2\sum_\nu b_\nu(d)^{3/2}.$$ The constant $C$ and the lower threshold for $N$ are independent of $\kappa,\eta,s$. Their dependence is only on the fixed phase, windows, and label bounds; all additional parameter dependence is contained in $P(\kappa,\eta)$ and $M(s)$.
  - Explanation: Linearity, the capacity decomposition, packet propagation, and weighted tube duality combine into the critical cubic estimate with explicit spatial and logarithmic losses.
- **claim-joint-sparse-ball-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-sparse)): There is a constant $\mu_0\in(0,1)$ depending only on the fixed graph setup with the following property. Suppose that $h$ is supported in $Q_0$, $\\|h\\|_3\leq1$, and $0<\mu\leq\mu_0$. Let $\mathcal B$ be a finite family of balls of common radius $R$ such that $$\#\mathcal B\leq\mu^{-8},\qquad R\geq\mu^{-100},\qquad \|x_B-x_{\widetilde B}\|\geq\mu^{-40}R \quad(B\ne\widetilde B),$$ where $x_B$ denotes the center of $B$. Choose a dyadic integer $N$ with $4R\leq N^2<16R$. For $0<\kappa<1/10$ and $0<\eta,s\leq1$, put $\gamma_0=10\kappa+\eta+3s/2$. Then $$\label{eq:sparse-estimate} \left\|\{\|F\|>\mu\}\cap\bigcup_{B\in\mathcal B}B\right\| \leq C\mu^{-3}P(\kappa,\eta)M(s)^{3/2} N^{\gamma_0}\log^2(2+N).$$ The constant $C$ and the threshold $\mu_0$ are independent of $\kappa,\eta,s$, of the centers, and of their maximum separation.
  - Explanation: Frequency excision and almost orthogonality provide a common data bound for all separated balls. Reconstruction and offset integration turn that coefficient estimate into a superlevel-measure estimate uniform in arbitrarily large separations.
- **claim-basic-convex-graph-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-basic)): Let $g\in L^2(\mathbb R^2)$ have compact support, and let $$G(y',t)=\int_{\mathbb R^2}g(\xi) e\big(y'\cdot\xi+t\phi(\xi)\big)\,d\xi.$$ If $\phi''\geq cI$ on $\mathbb R^2$, then $$\label{eq:elementary-lfour} \\|G\\|_{L^4(\mathbb R^3)} \leq (\pi/c)^{1/4}\\|g\\|_{L^2(\mathbb R^2)}.$$ In particular, for $F=E_\phi(h\chi_1^{\nu_*})$ with $h$ supported in $Q_0$, there is a fixed $C_{\mathrm b}\geq1$ such that $$\label{eq:basic-bounds} \\|F\\|_4+\\|F\\|_\infty+\\|\nabla F\\|_\infty \leq C_{\mathrm b}\\|h\\|_3.$$
  - Explanation: Uniform convexity controls the graph convolution density and hence the extension norm. Fixed compact frequency support also controls the function and its gradient uniformly.
- **claim-finite-superlevel-occupancy** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-occupancy)): There is a fixed $C_X\geq1$ such that at most $C_X\mu^{-7}$ unit cubes are occupied. In particular, if $\mu\leq C_X^{-1}$, one can choose one point of $A_\mu$ in each occupied cube to obtain a finite set $X$ satisfying $\#X\leq\mu^{-8}$.
  - Explanation: The gradient bound forces each occupied cube to contribute a controlled amount to the global fourth-power integral, so the occupied collection is finite.
- **claim-diameter-free-sparse-cover** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sparse-cover)): Let $0<\tau\leq1$, $0<\mu\leq\mu_0$, and let $X$ be the set in Lemma <a href="#lem:occupancy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:occupancy">12</a>. Set $$\label{eq:cover-scales} \beta=\frac{\tau}{4},\qquad J=\left\lceil\frac{32}{\tau}\right\rceil+1,\qquad R_j=\mu^{-100(j+1)}\quad(0\leq j\leq J).$$ The union of the occupied cubes is covered by at most $$\label{eq:family-count} 3J\bigl(1+8\log_2(1/\mu)\bigr)\mu^{-\beta}$$ families of balls. Each family has at most $\mu^{-8}$ balls, all of radius $R=4R_j$ for some $0\leq j<J$, and its centers are separated by at least $\mu^{-40}R$. For the dyadic $N$ chosen in Proposition <a href="#prop:sparse" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:sparse">10</a>, $$\label{eq:cover-N} 4R\leq N^2<16R,\qquad N<8\mu^{-50J}.$$
  - Explanation: Slow growth of local point counts, maximal disjoint balls, and greedy coloring produce sparse covering families without restricting absolute locations or the diameter of the point set.
- **claim-global-graph-extension** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-graph)): Fix a phase and window setup for which Theorem <a href="#thm:packet" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:packet">6</a> and Proposition <a href="#prop:sparse" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:sparse">10</a> apply. For $3<p\leq4$, put $$\label{eq:theta-choice} \tau=p-3,\qquad \theta=10^{-6}\tau^2.$$ There is a constant $C$, independent of $p$ in this interval, such that $$\label{eq:global-graph-bound} \\|E_\phi(h\chi_1^{\nu_*})\\|_p \leq C\left[ P(\theta,\theta)M(\theta)^{3/2}\tau^{-7} \right]^{1/p}\\|h\\|_3$$ for all $h\in L^3$ supported in $Q_0$. For $4<p<\infty$, Equation <a href="#eq:basic-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:basic-bounds">[eq:basic-bounds]</a> alone gives $\\|E_\phi(h\chi_1^{\nu_*})\\|_p\leq C_{\mathrm b}\\|h\\|_3$.
  - Explanation: The sparse cover absorbs the small spatial loss into a distribution estimate that is integrable above the critical exponent. Higher exponents follow directly from the elementary bounds.
- **claim-necessary-endpoint-divergence** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-lower)): For every nonempty compact smooth surface $\Sigma$ in the stated class there is $c_\Sigma>0$ such that its best extension constant satisfies $$\label{eq:constant-lower-bound} C_{\Sigma,p}^{\mathrm{best}} \geq c_\Sigma(p-3)^{-1/p},\qquad 3<p\leq4.$$ This conclusion does not use the Kakeya or packet estimates.
  - Explanation: A fixed interior smooth datum retains horizontal squared mass in a growing disk. Integrating its resulting lower bound in time forces divergence as the exponent approaches the critical endpoint.
- **claim-elliptic-bourgain-oscillatory-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-bourgain-oscillatory)): Let $$\phi(x,t;y)=x\cdot y+\psi(t;y),\qquad x,y\in\mathbb R^2,\quad t\in\mathbb R,\quad \psi(0;y)=0,$$ be a fixed real smooth phase near the origin. Suppose throughout this neighborhood that $$M(t,y)=D_y^2\partial_t\psi(t;y)\quad\hbox{is definite}, \qquad \partial_tM(t,y)=c(t,y)M(t,y)$$ for a smooth real scalar function $c$. On a sufficiently small fixed product chart, let $a$ be any fixed smooth amplitude with compact support in its interior. For every $3<p<\infty$, $$\label{eq:bourgain-oscillatory} \left\\|\int e^{iN\phi(x,t;y)}a(x,t;y)f(y)\,dy \right\\|_{L^p(\mathbb R^3_{x,t})} \le C_{\phi,a,p}N^{-3/p}\\|f\\|_{L^p(\mathbb R^2)},\qquad N\ge1,$$ where the operator is zero outside the output support of $a$. The constant may depend on the fixed chart, phase, amplitude and $p$, but not on $N$ or $f$.
  - Explanation: The scalar curvature evolution forces the scalar to depend only on time. An output change straightens the phase, and summable Fourier expansion of the amplitude reduces the estimate to surface extension with the stated scaling.

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Surface Fourier extension operator** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Its Fourier extension operator is $$E_\Sigma f(x)=\int_\Sigma f(\xi)e^{2\pi i x\cdot\xi}\,d\sigma(\xi).$$
- **Positive curvature** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Positive curvature means that the second fundamental form is definite at every point, with the normal chosen on each chart so that the form is positive definite.
- **Inhomogeneous Bessel-potential space** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Let $W^{s,p}(\mathbb R^2)$ denote the inhomogeneous Bessel-potential space with norm $\\|f\\|_{W^{s,p}}=\\|(I-\Delta)^{s/2}f\\|_{L^p(\mathbb R^2)}$.
- **Exponential phase notation** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Write $e(t)=\exp(2\pi i t)$.
- **Elliptic capacity and packet gauge** ([source](#pm-h-10)): For a finite array $c$ in one bin at the scale $(\theta,r,w)$ define $$\begin{aligned} K_{\theta,r}(c) &=\sup_{\substack{E,u,v\\L\ge a\ge\theta}} \frac{\theta^2}{W_\kappa(E)} \sum_{\substack{U_\nu\in u+E\\z\in v+rE}}\|c_{\nu,z}\|^2, \label{eq:packet-general-capacity}\\ G_{\theta,r}(c)^3 &=\left(w^2\sum_{\nu,z}\|c_{\nu,z}\|^2\right) K_{\theta,r}(c)^{1/2}. \label{eq:packet-gauge} \end{aligned}$$
- **Atomic domination norm** ([source](#pm-h-10)): On each finite index set let $$\begin{aligned} A_{\theta,r}(c)&= \inf\left\{\sum_{\ell=1}^qG_{\theta,r}(a_\ell): \|c\|\le\sum_{\ell=1}^q\|a_\ell\|,\ q<\infty\right\},\\ \mathcal A_m(c)^3&=r\sum_I A_{\theta,r}(c_I)^3. \end{aligned}$$
- **Positive weighted tube density** ([source](#sec:density)): For an initial array $d=(d_{\nu,z})$, define $$\label{eq:tube-function} \begin{split} T_{\nu,z} &=\{(X,t)\in\mathbb R^2\times\mathbb R: 0\le t\le1,\ \|X-z-tU_\nu\|\le\delta\},\\ H_d(X,t)&=\sum_{\nu,z}\|d_{\nu,z}\|^2\mathbf1_{T_{\nu,z}}(X,t), \qquad b_\nu(d)=\sum_z\|d_{\nu,z}\|^2. \end{split}$$
- **Kakeya maximal operator** ([source](#sec:density)): For clarity, the precise maximal operator used below is $$K_\rho g(\omega)=\sup_{a\in\mathbb R^3} \frac{1}{\pi\rho^2}\int_{\mathcal T_\rho(a,\omega)}\|g(y)\|\,dy, \qquad \mathcal T_\rho(a,\omega) =\{a+r\omega+w: \|r\|\le\tfrac12,\ w\perp\omega,\ \|w\|\le\rho\}.$$
- **Superlevel set and occupied cube** ([source](#pm-h-15)): Call such a cube occupied if it meets $A_\mu$.

      "exact_definition": "For an initial array $d=(d_{\\nu,z})$, define $$\\label{eq:tube-function}\n\\begin{split}\n T_{\\nu,z}\n   &=\\{(X,t)\\in\\mathbb R^2\\times\\mathbb R:\n                   0\\le t\\le1,\\ |X-z-tU_\\nu|\\le\\delta\\},\\\\\n H_d(X,t)&=\\sum_{\\nu,z}|d_{\\nu,z}|^2\\mathbf1_{T_{\\nu,z}}(X,t),\n \\qquad b_\\nu(d)=\\sum_z|d_{\\nu,z}|^2.\n\\end{split}$$",
      "interpretation": "Assigns nonnegative squared coefficient weights to moving tubes and sums position weights within each direction label.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Positive weighted tube density",
      "scope": "Fixed bounded initial frequency and position label ranges."
    },

| q-loss-removal-parameter | \theta | = | $10^{-6}\tau^2$ | The parameter is chosen using the distance of the output exponent from the critical endpoint. |
| q-layer-cake-integral | \int_0^1\mu^{-1+\tau/2}\bigl(1+\log(1/\mu)\bigr)^3\,d\mu | = | $\frac2\tau+\frac{12}{\tau^2} +\frac{48}{\tau^3}+\frac{96}{\tau^4}$ | Positive distance from the critical exponent in the stated bounded exponent interval. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-insert-elliptic-charts** [claim](#stmt-lem-continuation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-continuation): Match the graph Taylor data with a continued spherical phase in its transition annulus, then absorb a localized perturbation into the cutoff. -> A fixed affine normalization and admissible continued phase agreeing with the target graph locally.
- **step-exact-frame-normalization** [claim](#stmt-lem-packet-frame) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-packet-frame): Apply Fourier inversion and Parseval on each square window, then sum the square partition. -> Exact reconstruction and analysis and synthesis norm identities.
- **step-atomic-cubic-domination** [claim](#stmt-lem-packet-atomic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-packet-atomic): Use independently translated disk tests and the triangle inequality for each atomic domination. -> A monotone atomic norm controlling the weighted cubic sample sum.
- **step-specialize-packet-propagation** [claim](#stmt-thm-packet) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-packet): Use the cited propagation theorem at the terminal factor and apply the atomic cubic lower bound; absorb finitely many small scales. -> The masked cubic sample estimate for arbitrary finite complex input arrays.
- **step-dualize-kakeya-maximal** [claim](#stmt-lem-weighted) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weighted): Separate direction caps, cover graph tubes by boundedly many cylinders, and dualize the maximal estimate. -> The weighted tube-density estimate with uniform geometric constants.
- **step-group-capacity-remainders** [claim](#stmt-lem-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-density): Remove bundles selected by near-maximal ellipse tests, group removals by remainder capacity, and bound the terminal remainder by a largest coefficient. -> Disjoint logarithmically many pieces with total capacity cost bounded by tube density.
- **step-merge-critical-discrete-bound** [claim](#stmt-prop-discrete) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-discrete): Use linearity and finite-sum inequalities to sum propagated capacity pieces, then insert the weighted tube estimate. -> A discrete cubic estimate with the combined small spatial loss and logarithmic factor.
- **step-joint-sparse-balls** [claim](#stmt-prop-sparse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-sparse): Excise resonant frequencies, bound the joint Gram operator by integration by parts, control reconstruction tails, and integrate a common sample offset. -> Uniform superlevel control over the whole family using a common data norm.
- **step-basic-convex-estimates** [claim](#stmt-lem-basic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-basic): Control graph convolution by a radial substitution using uniform convexity, apply Plancherel, and differentiate the compactly supported integral. -> The elementary extension bound and fixed uniform function and gradient bounds.
- **step-finite-occupancy** [claim](#stmt-lem-occupancy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-occupancy): Use the gradient bound to thicken selected superlevel points and compare their bounded-overlap contributions with the global fourth-power integral. -> A finite representative point set with the stated cardinality bound.
- **step-diameter-free-cover** [claim](#stmt-lem-sparse-cover) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sparse-cover): Find slow-growth scales, group by local counts, select maximal disjoint balls, and greedily color the center graph. -> Controlled sparse ball families covering all occupied cubes with bounded scale growth.
- **step-integrate-global-distribution** [claim](#stmt-thm-graph) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-graph): Choose small loss exponents from the output exponent, sum the sparse cover, and integrate the distribution estimate by layer cake. -> The global graph extension estimate and its explicit elementary exponent dependence.
- **step-fixed-datum-lower-bound** [claim](#stmt-prop-lower) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-lower): Use horizontal Plancherel and a derivative moment to retain squared mass in a growing disk, apply Hölder, and integrate in time. -> Necessary divergence of the best extension constant near the critical exponent.
- **step-transfer-to-compact-surface** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#pm-h-20): Use a measurable finite chart partition, retain surface and physical Jacobians, apply the graph estimates, and use finite-measure embedding. -> Diagonal surface extension, including boundary charts and rough data.
- **step-schrodinger-frequency-transfer** [claim](#stmt-cor-schrodinger-smoothing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-lower-2): Apply the surface estimate to the compact paraboloid, invoke compact-frequency transfer, and sum frequency pieces using strict Sobolev regularity. -> Bounded free Schrödinger evolution from the stated Sobolev space to local-in-time spacetime norm.
- **step-straighten-oscillatory-phase** [claim](#stmt-cor-bourgain-oscillatory) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-bourgain-oscillatory): Use Hessian compatibility to remove spatial dependence of the scalar curvature factor, straighten output variables, and sum the amplitude Fourier expansion. -> The local oscillatory-integral estimate with the scaling factor and no power loss.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-diagonal-surface-bound**: The Fourier extension operator is bounded between the stated surface and ambient Lebesgue spaces for each fixed surface and exponent in the stated open range, including surfaces with smooth boundary.
- **invariant-atomic-mask-contraction**: The atomic norm is monotone under absolute coordinatewise domination and cannot increase under a coordinate mask.
- **invariant-smoothing-regularity**: The free Schrödinger propagator has the stated spacetime bound when the exponent and Sobolev order satisfy the strict inequalities in the corollary.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-surface-class-expansion**: Apply the extension estimate to an arbitrary surface solely because an oscillatory integral can be formed on it. -> The theorem supplies no estimate for a surface outside its compactness, smoothness, and positive-curvature hypotheses.
- **anti-endpoint-extension**: Treat the stated extension bound as a finite bound at the critical endpoint. -> The theorem states an open exponent range, and the lower bound forces the best constant to diverge as that endpoint is approached on a nonempty surface.
- **anti-endpoint-sobolev-summation**: Replace the strict Sobolev regularity condition by equality in the smoothing consequence. -> The proof uses convergence of a frequency-scale series that requires a strict regularity surplus.
- **anti-finite-frame-certificate**: Certify the exact frame identities for arbitrary data by checking a finite truncation of packet coefficients. -> Finite partial sums do not establish the limiting identities or exact reconstruction for all admissible data.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result bounds a continuous Fourier extension operator on an infinite-dimensional function space rather than specifying a finite executable construction. At stmt-thm-main the source states: For every $3<p<\infty$, there is a finite constant $C_{\Sigma,p}$ such that $$\label{eq:main} \\|E_\Sigma f\\|_{L^p(\mathbb R^3)} \le C_{\Sigma,p}\\|f\\|_{L^p(\Sigma)}$$ for every complex-valued $f\in L^p(\Sigma)$. Finite coordinate arrays in intermediate lemmas do not remove the continuous capacity tests or the function-space limits. At proof-stmt-lem-packet-frame the source states: These identities first hold for finite partial Fourier sums in $L^2$ and then for their limits. A finite test suite therefore cannot decide the stated analytic invariants, and the supplied source does not give a finite exact evaluation procedure for the capacity and atomic norm.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The extension estimate is an analytic operator bound. It supplies neither a finite representation of arbitrary surface data nor an executable evaluation procedure with certified error. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The free Schrödinger consequence can inform analytic expectations for dispersive evolution, but its spacetime estimate does not construct a time integrator or control discretization error. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The packet interface allows coordinate masks and finite arrays, but its estimate depends on analytic capacity and phase hypotheses. The paper does not provide a finite certification procedure for those hypotheses or for the packet constant. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The sparse-cover proof uses finite point counts, maximal disjoint ball selection, a proximity graph, and greedy coloring. Its input set consists of points selected from occupied cubes of an analytic superlevel set. The paper does not supply an executable procedure for obtaining that input from arbitrary rough graph data, a finite encoding of its geometric parameters, or certified distance comparisons. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite graph is an auxiliary representation used to separate ball centers. The paper gives no storage, indexing, update, or query contract for a reusable data structure. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The exact frame identities concern continuous functions and their packet coefficients. They do not prescribe a finite transform architecture, an arithmetic format, or accelerator resource requirements. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The weighted tube argument controls nonnegative geometric concentration by analytic duality. It supplies no adversarial model, cryptographic construction, or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The atomic norm controls packet coefficients under coordinate domination and masks. The paper does not construct a learning objective, training procedure, or statistical generalization guarantee from this norm. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The capacity decomposition chooses approximately maximizing ellipse tests, but the proof does not give an effective optimization routine for finding those tests over unrestricted orientations, translations, and semiaxes. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Uniformity in ball locations and large geometric separations concerns oscillatory analysis. It does not define communication, synchronization, consistency, or failure handling among distributed processes. |
| other (Harmonic analysis and dispersive partial differential equations) | False | none | not_applicable | stated | The paper supplies analytic extension and smoothing estimates, together with a route from packet capacity and weighted tubes to global graph bounds. These are mathematical contracts that require further work before they become finite engineering constructions. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-surface-geometric-class** (hypothesis): The diagonal surface estimate requires compactness, smoothness, and positive curvature; smooth boundary is permitted. Blocked use: ['Apply the theorem directly to a noncompact, nonsmooth, flat, or indefinite-curvature surface.']
- **limit-open-exponent-range** (edge_case): The surface estimate is stated only for finite exponents strictly above the critical endpoint. Its best constant necessarily diverges toward that endpoint on a nonempty surface. Blocked use: ['Assert a finite critical-endpoint bound from the stated theorem.', 'Claim a constant uniform as the exponent approaches the critical endpoint.']
- **limit-packet-continuation-class** (hypothesis): Uniform convexity suffices for the elementary estimates but does not alone establish applicability of the imported packet theorem. The specific continuation form must be verified. Blocked use: ['Invoke packet propagation for an arbitrary uniformly convex phase without establishing the required continuation form.']
- **limit-fixed-packet-complexity** (formal_scope): Packet propagation requires a fixed admissible complexity range and fixed phase and window setup. The single-step map retains the full matrix between the input and retained output indices. Blocked use: ['Allow label bounds or complexity assumptions to vary without accounting for changes in the packet constant.', 'Replace the full packet matrix by an additional sparsification and claim the same estimate without proof.']
- **limit-frame-identities-and-truncation** (approximation): The exact frame identities extend through convergence in the function space. A finite coefficient truncation is not itself exact reconstruction for arbitrary admissible data. Blocked use: ['Treat a finite partial Fourier sum as exact reconstruction for arbitrary input.', 'Infer a finite-precision or truncation-error guarantee solely from the exact frame identities.']
- **limit-weighted-tube-input** (hypothesis): The weighted tube estimate depends on the imported maximal estimate, the fixed bounded geometric setup, sufficiently fine dyadic scale, and a positive permitted loss exponent. Blocked use: ['Use the weighted estimate with a vanishing loss exponent.', 'Remove the lower scale threshold without proving the required cylinder-radius condition.', 'Extend the argument to an unbounded velocity range with the same geometric constants.']
- **limit-capacity-selection-effectivity** (nonconstructive): The density decomposition terminates on finite arrays, but its selection step requires an approximately maximizing continuous ellipse test. The proof does not supply a finite search procedure for that choice. Blocked use: ['Describe the existence proof as an implemented capacity optimizer.', 'Restrict ellipse orientations, translations, or semiaxes to a finite grid without proving preservation of the required density approximation.', 'Extend the finite removal argument directly to infinitely supported arrays.']
- **limit-discrete-map-and-loss** (formal_scope): The discrete cubic estimate concerns the specified synthesis and sampling map with a fixed permitted output mask. It retains spatial and logarithmic losses and does not bound arbitrary array transformations. Blocked use: ['Apply the estimate to an unrelated terminal array or arbitrary linear map.', 'Drop the displayed spatial and logarithmic factors before the separate loss-removal argument.']
- **limit-sparse-family-geometry** (hypothesis): The joint sparse-ball estimate requires normalized compactly supported graph data and a finite common-radius family satisfying the cardinality, radius, and center-separation inequalities. Blocked use: ['Apply the joint estimate to densely clustered balls.', 'Use independently normalized data on different balls while claiming the common data-norm conclusion.', 'Omit the small-level or scale-comparability conditions.']
- **limit-excision-and-reconstruction-errors** (approximation): The sparse-ball proof separately controls discarded resonant frequencies and the omitted spatial coefficient tail. Rough data and the sharp excision boundary are not differentiated. Blocked use: ['Discard resonances without retaining the uniform error estimate.', 'Identify truncated synthesized samples with the original extension without accounting for both approximation stages.', 'Integrate by parts through the rough datum or the excision indicator.']
- **limit-occupancy-normalization** (hypothesis): Finite occupancy uses the elementary global norm and gradient bounds for normalized compactly supported graph data. The stronger representative cardinality bound also requires the stated smallness threshold. Blocked use: ['Transfer the occupied-cube count unchanged to unnormalized data.', 'Claim the stronger representative bound for levels outside its threshold.', 'Interpret finite occupancy as a finite procedure for discovering the superlevel set of arbitrary rough data.']
- **limit-sparse-cover-input-and-boundaries** (formal_scope): The finite covering construction assumes the representative point set and admissible geometric parameters are supplied. It uses open balls and a strict graph adjacency threshold, with no diameter restriction. Blocked use: ['Apply the family-count guarantee to an arbitrary point set without its cardinality and parameter conditions.', 'Change open-ball counting or strict adjacency conventions without rechecking boundary cases.', 'Claim that the covering construction evaluates the continuous extension operator.']
- **limit-input-constant-growth** (unknown): The elementary exponent losses are explicit, but the full extension constant has no supplied polynomial growth bound near the critical exponent because the imported input constants have no supplied growth rates. Blocked use: ['Report the elementary loss factor as a polynomial bound for the full operator norm.', 'Assign numerical values or asymptotic rates to the packet and maximal constants without additional evidence.']
- **limit-surface-transfer-jacobians** (formal_scope): Passage from graph data to surface data retains the surface density, the physical affine determinant, and finite-measure embedding. Smoothness of the datum or its zero extension is not required. Blocked use: ['Identify graph and surface norms while omitting their Jacobian factors.', 'Demand smooth zero extension across a boundary as an additional theorem hypothesis.', 'Claim a uniform estimate across changing surfaces from constants attached to fixed charts.']
- **limit-schrodinger-regularity-and-time** (hypothesis): The smoothing consequence concerns free Schrödinger evolution in the stated spatial dimension and local time interval, with strict exponent and Sobolev-order inequalities. Blocked use: ['Replace strict Sobolev regularity by equality.', 'Claim the same estimate for a nonlinear evolution or an unrestricted time interval.', 'Infer stability or error control for a numerical time integrator.']
- **limit-oscillatory-curvature-evolution** (hypothesis): The oscillatory-integral consequence requires a fixed real smooth translation-invariant phase, definite curvature evolving by a scalar multiple, and a sufficiently small fixed product chart with an interior compactly supported smooth amplitude. Blocked use: ['Invoke the scaling estimate using ellipticity alone.', 'Allow the phase, amplitude, or chart to vary with the oscillation parameter while retaining the same constant.', 'Extend the local conclusion to outputs outside the amplitude support.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the diagonal Fourier extension conjecture for compact smooth positively curved surfaces $`\Sigma\subset\mathbb R^3`$, including surfaces with smooth boundary. For every $`3\lt p\lt \infty`$, the extension operator maps $`L^p(\Sigma)`$ boundedly into $`L^p(\mathbb R^3)`$. The compact paraboloid case also yields free Schrödinger local smoothing in two spatial dimensions for every $`3\lt p\lt \infty`$ and Sobolev order $`s\gt 2-6/p`$.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Let $\Sigma\subset\mathbb R^3$ be a compact smooth surface, with induced surface measure $d\sigma$. Its Fourier extension operator is $$E_\Sigma f(x)=\int_\Sigma f(\xi)e^{2\pi i x\cdot\xi}\,d\sigma(\xi).$$ We allow smooth boundary. Positive curvature means that the second fundamental form is definite at every point, with the normal chosen on each chart so that the form is positive definite.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:main"></a>
**Theorem 1**. *Let $\Sigma\subset\mathbb R^3$ be a compact smooth positively curved surface, possibly with smooth boundary. For every $3<p<\infty$, there is a finite constant $C_{\Sigma,p}$ such that $$\label{eq:main}
 \|E_\Sigma f\|_{L^p(\mathbb R^3)}
 \le C_{\Sigma,p}\|f\|_{L^p(\Sigma)}$$ for every complex-valued $f\in L^p(\Sigma)$.*

</div>

The theorem establishes the diagonal extension conjecture in three dimensions for positively curved surfaces. It includes the sphere, compact elliptic paraboloid patches, and general smooth elliptic graphs. The surface and the exponent are fixed throughout each estimate.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
###### Background.

The restriction problem originates in Stein’s work; the Tomas–Stein theory gives $L^2(\Sigma)\to L^4(\mathbb R^3)$ for positively curved surfaces (Tomas 1975; Stein 1979) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Tomas1975](#ref-Tomas1975), [Stein1979](#ref-Stein1979)]. The bilinear framework of Tao, Vargas, and Vega (Tao, Vargas, and Vega 1998) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[TaoVargasVega1998](#ref-TaoVargasVega1998)], Wolff’s cone estimate (Wolff 2001) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Wolff2001](#ref-Wolff2001)], and Tao’s paraboloid estimate (Tao 2003) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Tao2003](#ref-Tao2003)] developed the role of wave packets and transverse interactions. Bennett, Carbery, and Tao’s multilinear estimates (Bennett, Carbery, and Tao 2006) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[BennettCarberyTao2006](#ref-BennettCarberyTao2006)] and Bourgain–Guth’s approach (Bourgain and Guth 2011) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[BourgainGuth2011](#ref-BourgainGuth2011)] further connected these interactions to the linear problem.

Guth’s polynomial-partitioning argument established the bounded-data estimate for $p>13/4$ (Guth 2016, Theorem 0.1) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Guth2016](#ref-Guth2016)]. Shayya obtained diagonal estimates in that range (Shayya 2017, Corollary 2.1(iii)) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Shayya2017](#ref-Shayya2017)]; see also Kim’s treatment (Kim 2017, Theorem 1.2 and the following remark) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Kim2017](#ref-Kim2017)]. Wang’s broom method further improved bounded-data restriction for the truncated paraboloid (Wang 2022, Theorem 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Wang2022Brooms](#ref-Wang2022Brooms)]. Wang and Wu proved the diagonal bound for $p>22/7$ on compact positively curved surfaces, including surfaces with boundary, using decoupling and two-ends Furstenberg inequalities (Wang and Wu 2024, Theorem 0.2) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[WangWu2024](#ref-WangWu2024)].

For the sphere and quadratic graphs, factorization gives a separate route from bounded-data estimates to diagonal estimates with an arbitrarily small increase in the exponent; see Bourgain (Bourgain 1991) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Bourgain1991Besicovitch](#ref-Bourgain1991Besicovitch)] and Buschenhenke (Buschenhenke 2024, Theorem 1.1 and Remark 1.6) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Buschenhenke2024](#ref-Buschenhenke2024)]. The argument here treats general smooth elliptic charts through the packet theorem’s full array formulation and the explicit phase continuation. The packet and maximal inputs play distinct roles: the former controls oscillatory coefficients, while the latter controls their positive tube density.

The compact paraboloid case also gives a consequence for free Schrödinger evolution. Let $W^{s,p}(\mathbb R^2)$ denote the inhomogeneous Bessel-potential space with norm $\|f\|_{W^{s,p}}=\|(I-\Delta)^{s/2}f\|_{L^p(\mathbb R^2)}$.

<a id="stmt-cor-schrodinger-smoothing"></a>
<div id="cor:schrodinger-smoothing" class="corollary">

<a id="eq:schrodinger-smoothing"></a>
**Corollary 2** (Free Schrödinger local smoothing). *For every $3<p<\infty$ and $s>2-6/p$, the free Schrödinger propagator, initially defined on Schwartz functions, extends boundedly from $W^{s,p}(\mathbb R^2)$ to $L^p(\mathbb R^2\times[0,1])$: $$\label{eq:schrodinger-smoothing}
 \|e^{it\Delta}f\|_{L^p(\mathbb R^2\times[0,1])}
 \le C_{p,s}\|f\|_{W^{s,p}(\mathbb R^2)}.$$*

</div>

The spacetime norm is global in space and local in time. Rogers’s necessary condition (Rogers 2008, sec. 2) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Rogers2008](#ref-Rogers2008)] shows that the Sobolev threshold cannot be lowered. We deduce the corollary from the compact-frequency transfer of Lee, Rogers, and Seeger (S. Lee, Rogers, and Seeger 2013, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[LeeRogersSeeger2013](#ref-LeeRogersSeeger2013)] in Section <a href="#sec:surfaces" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:surfaces">7</a>.

A second consequence concerns local oscillatory integrals with phase $N[x\cdot y+\psi(t;y)]$, where $x,y\in\mathbb R^2$, $t\in\mathbb R$, and the amplitude is smooth and compactly supported. Suppose $D_y^2\partial_t\psi$ is definite and its time derivative is a scalar multiple of itself. A change of output variables then reduces the phase to a fixed elliptic graph. Corollary <a href="#cor:bourgain-oscillatory" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:bourgain-oscillatory">16</a> gives the resulting $L^p$ estimate with the scaling factor $N^{-3/p}$ for every finite $p>3$. The additional phase condition is the translation-invariant form of Bourgain’s condition; we explain the straightening directly in Section <a href="#sec:surfaces" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:surfaces">7</a>, following the setting of Gao–Liu–Xi (Gao, Liu, and Xi 2025) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[GaoLiuXi2025](#ref-GaoLiuXi2025)].

The proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> uses two theorem inputs. The packet propagation theorem (OpenAI 2026a, Theorem 6.4) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[OpenAIPacket2026](#ref-OpenAIPacket2026)] controls arbitrary finite complex coefficient arrays through an elliptic capacity, with coordinate masks permitted. The Kakeya maximal theorem (OpenAI 2026b, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[OpenAIKakeya2026](#ref-OpenAIKakeya2026)] controls nonnegative weighted tube concentration. We state both inputs in the forms used here and verify their hypotheses. The remainder of the proof gives the density conversion, the estimates for rough $L^3$ graph data, and the passage to general surfaces.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
###### The argument.

After fixing a graph chart, an explicit continuation places its phase in the class allowed by the packet theorem. The construction matches the quadratic Taylor data in a transition annulus of a continued spherical patch and preserves uniform ellipticity. It applies equally to boundary charts, where the graph extends smoothly and the datum is extended by zero.

The packet theorem gives a cubic sample estimate with a small spatial power loss and an elliptic-capacity factor. A decomposition according to the capacity of the remaining coefficients allows this factor to be summed using the positive weighted tube estimate. The latter follows from the Kakeya maximal theorem by duality; its direction and position formulation follows the classical weighted tube framework of Tao, Vargas, and Vega (Tao, Vargas, and Vega 1998, arXiv version 1, Section 3) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[TaoVargasVega1998](#ref-TaoVargasVega1998)].

To pass to $L^3$ data, we prove one joint estimate for a sparse family of balls. Removing a small union of frequency resonances makes the corresponding packet analyses almost orthogonal. The bound is uniform in the ball locations and in arbitrarily large separations. A covering of each level set then removes the spatial loss. This follows Tao’s sparse-ball strategy for epsilon removal (Tao 1999) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Tao1999Epsilon](#ref-Tao1999Epsilon)]; see the detailed treatments in Bourgain–Guth’s appendix (Bourgain and Guth 2011) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[BourgainGuth2011](#ref-BourgainGuth2011)] and Kim’s appendix (Kim 2017) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Kim2017](#ref-Kim2017)]. We prove the precise covering and summation needed for the present estimate.

The resulting graph bound has $L^3$ input and $L^p$ output for every $p>3$. A finite chart decomposition, with the surface and physical-space Jacobians retained, gives Equation <a href="#eq:main" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:main">[eq:main]</a> by the finite-measure embedding $L^p(\Sigma)\subset L^3(\Sigma)$.

Figure <a href="#fig:argument-map" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:argument-map">1</a> records the two inputs and the successive reductions.

<figure id="fig:argument-map">

<figcaption>The two independent inputs meet in the sampled cubic estimate. The sparse-ball bound then supplies the common $L^{3}$ control needed for globalization and passage to the surface.</figcaption>
</figure>
